# Devin autonomous task list — DOCX/DrawingML rendering fidelity

This file is the task queue for the Devin CLI task loop
(`.devin/taskloop.sh`). Each run of the loop picks the FIRST row whose
Status is `pending` or `in_progress` (crash-restart), works it to
completion, updates the row, commits, and stops.

Status values: `pending` | `in_progress` | `done` | `blocked`

Rules for the agent working this file:
- Mark the row `in_progress` before starting work, `done` only after
  building and verifying per the task's own verification hint.
- `blocked:<reason>` in Notes when genuinely stuck — never fake done.
- One task per run: after committing, STOP.
- Never revert unrelated working-tree changes.
- Append a one-line summary to `.devin/WORKLOG.md` when a task completes.

Context: repo is a GTK4 AbiWord fork (Abinova 4.0.0). The OOXML
importer lives in `src/wp/impexp/openxml/`, frame layout/render in
`src/text/fmt/xp/fl_FrameLayout.cpp` + `src/text/fmt/xp/fp_FrameContainer.cpp`,
text runs in `fp_TextRun*` + `gr_CairoGraphics.cpp` (Pango), the abwn
writer/reader in `src/wp/impexp/xp/ie_{exp,imp}_Abinova_1.cpp`, props in
`src/text/ptbl/xp/pp_Property.cpp`. Build: `cd src && make -j2` —
note that vendored libs live outside src/, so after touching
`thirdparty/wv-1.2.9` run `make -C thirdparty` first (src make alone
links stale objects).
Headless render check: `src/abinova --to=pdf --to-name=out.pdf file.abwn`
then `pdftoppm -png -r 72 out.pdf out`. Reference docx files live in
`~/Documents/*.docx` (Austin, Badge, banded, Crop, facet, feathered,
filgree, headiness, integral, ion-dark, ion-light, retrospect, semaphore,
slice-dark, slice-light, viewmaster, whip); generated cover fragments in
`src/wp/covers/*.xml` (regenerate with `tools/mkcovers.py`).

driving the vendored wvWare parser in `thirdparty/wv-1.2.9/` (wv.h,
wvparse.c, sprm.c, fib.c, etc.). Base all semantics on the official
MS-DOC spec (downloaded reference: /tmp/ms-doc.docx, spec name
[MS-DOC] v260217 — sections 2.1 streams/storages, 2.2 CP/PLC/sprm,
2.4 text/tables/properties, 2.5 FIB + FibRgFcLcb*, 2.8 stylesheet,
lists, OfficeArt blips).

## EPUB import/export modernization

EPUB lives in core at `src/wp/impexp/epub/` — importer `imp/xp/ie_imp_EPUB.cpp`
(~450 lines, old plugin-era code) and exporter `exp/xp/ie_exp_EPUB.cpp` +
`ie_exp_EPUB_EPUB3Writer.cpp` (~170 lines). EPUB spec: OCF container ->
META-INF/container.xml -> OPF package doc (metadata/manifest/spine/guide) ->
XHTML content docs + NCX (EPUB2) or nav.xhtml (EPUB3).

| ID | Task | Status | Attempts | Notes |
|----|------|--------|----------|-------|

Wire the open call, then verify a saved .mht imports text+images. | done | 1 | _loadFile now calls stream.open(input) (BOGUSDOCUMENT on failure); open() rewinds the input (gsf_input_seek 0) and does a defensive chunked read of gsf_input_remaining so sniff-consumed/short reads can't truncate the archive — synthetic multipart/related .mht (xhtml part + base64 PNG via Content-Location) -> PDF shows text + red image; malformed multipart-only .mht rejects cleanly to text fallback; Badge.docx regression OK |
| M02 | Fix MHT body decoding: importMultipart reads part bodies line-by-line and append() injects '\n' per line — corrupts raw (non-encoded) binary parts; append_Quoted reads 2 chars past bufend on trailing '=' (over-read); base64 accumulation edge cases. Decode per Content-Transfer-Encoding over the raw part body, not re-wrapped lines. Verify: .mht with raw+base64+QP images imports all correctly. | done | 1 | UT_MHTStream::nextBody extracts the raw part body byte-range (boundary must be a lone line-start delimiter, CRLF before delimiter excluded per RFC 2046 5.1.1); append() decodes per CTE over the whole body — raw parts stored verbatim (no '\n' injection/CR stripping), append_Quoted rewritten bounds-safe (truncated '=X'/trailing '=' at EOF emitted literally, soft breaks incl LF-only honored, stray g_try_malloc/80-byte leak gone), append_Base64 honors decode failure + end-of-body flush; parseHeaders rewinds on a boundary so parts lacking the blank line aren't swallowed. Verified: synthetic .mht -> PDF shows raw-8bit PNG (IDAT with literal CRLFs — CRC proves byte-exact), base64 PNG + QP PNG; QP doc part decodes =XX + soft breaks; edge .mht (fake boundary line, empty body, no blank line, closing delim) imports; valgrind 0 errors; Badge.docx->pdf regression OK |
| M03 | MHT export verify + CID matching: check _createMultipart output (boundary termination, quoted-printable path is commented out at ie_exp_HTML.cpp:664); fix contentID '<>'/whitespace handling and contentLocation fallback in importImage. Verify: save .mht -> reimport roundtrip keeps images. | done | 1 | export multipart rewritten RFC-clean: MULTIPART_FIELD emits "Name: value" (was "Name : value"), each part opens its own "--boundary" line at line start (was glued to previous part's delimiter so only the first part parsed), _createMultipart writes the "--boundary--" closer, Date header uses C-locale English names + real %z zone (was localized "cs, 01 okt" + hardcoded +0100); importer: recognizeContents returns PERFECT when the buffer's RFC-822 header block declares multipart/related+xhtml (Markdown sniffer scored 216 vs MHT 170 on our own exports and stole them to text), importImage url-decodes refs and compares full normalized Content-IDs (strip <>, trim inside+outside brackets, case-insensitive cid:) with Content-Location exact + absolute-ends-in-relative fallback — verified: filgree.docx -> .mht -> .abwn keeps both images (mht now parsed by MHT importer, parts separated + closed), synthetic 5-case CID/location fixture (bracketed, bare, spaced+%20, absolute-vs-relative, exact) imports all 5, real-PNG .mht -> PDF renders image, markdown/.mht sniffer regression OK |

## C++ audit / code-quality sweep

Systematic audit of `src/` (plus `thirdparty/wv-1.2.9` where the bug
class applies — it is C, so C++-only items like shared_ptr/exceptions/
variant apply to src/ only). One bug class per row: find real
instances, fix them, verify `cd src && make -j2` (rebuild
`make -C thirdparty` first if wv was touched) + a headless convert
smoke test. Rules: fix concrete defects, not style — no mass
reformatting; if a class yields a huge hit list, fix the dangerous
cases and list the rest in Notes. Keep sanitizer/valgrind runs in
small batches — the loop's 2048MB RSS watchdog killed full-corpus ASan
runs before (see DOC19–DOC21 — the monolithic version was
watchdog-killed 10 times and split).

Verify: build + audit mutex users. | done | 1 | audit: zero live locking in the whole tree — the only GMutex lives in UT_MutexImpl behind UT_Mutex (zero call sites); no std::mutex/GCond/condition_variable/atomic/GAsyncQueue/g_once/flock/gdk_threads anywhere in src/ or thirdparty; single worker thread (update-check GTask) shares no state and takes no locks; no nested gtk_main/gtk_dialog_run, no g_main_context_invoke — no multi-mutex path exists so no ordering can invert; UT_Worker/Timer/Idle are all main-loop sources. ### MHTML follow-ups

| ID | Task | Status | Attempts | Notes |
|----|------|--------|----------|-------|
| M04 | Reduce MHTML import memory: ie_imp_MHT.cpp loads the whole file into UT_MHTStream::m_data, copies every retained part body into a std::string via nextBody, then decodes into m_buf (~3x file size peak). Two bounded wins: (1) LAZY DECODE — importMultipart decodes every image part at load even if never referenced; store raw body + CTE and decode inside importImage (which already detachBuffer()s the decoded buf — the hook is half there). (2) STREAM — feed UT_MHTStream from the GsfInput incrementally (boundaries are line-based) or decode nextBody slices in place instead of the extra std::string copy. Verify: make -j2 clean; import a large .mht (10+ MB, many images) — all referenced images resolve, unreferenced images no longer decoded, peak RSS measurably lower than before. | done | 1 | both wins landed: UT_MHTStream never slurps the file — readLine serves header lines from an 8KB read-ahead window, and bodies are located by scanBoundary (chunked '\n'-at-line-start scan + matchAt tail check preserving the RFC 2046 preceding-CRLF rule, empty-body/no-blank-line and rest-of-archive malformed cases); nextBody returns (offset,len) ranges into the input, which the importer refs for its lifetime (UT_GsfInputPtr). importMultipart just stores the range via setBodyRange; new loadPartBody materialises+CTE-decodes on demand — doc part before parse, images inside importImage — so unreferenced parts are never read, decoded or copied at all; capped at UT_MAX_ARCHIVE_MEMBER_SIZE. Verified: make -j2 clean, make check PASS; 20MB .mht (6 referenced + 6 unreferenced 1.2MB PNGs): peak RSS 112228->85652KB (-24%), all 6 refs render pixel-identical; 10-fixture A/B vs pre-change binary: PDF text/images/pages identical (PNG renders pixel-identical); valgrind 0 errors/0 definite leaks; docx->mht->pdf round-trip keeps images+smasks |
| M05 | Support nested multipart in MHTML import (real-world interop gap): Outlook/Word .mht files nest multipart/alternative inside multipart/related, but UT_MHTStream::nextPart tracks only ONE boundary level — an HTML part nested one level deeper is never reached, so the import returns UT_IE_BOGUSDOCUMENT. Make the part walk recursive (each part with a multipart/* Content-Type re-parses its own boundary) or flatten nested boundaries into the part list. Add a nested-boundary .mht fixture if none exists. Verify: make -j2 clean; a multipart/alternative-in-related .mht (or a Word/Outlook-generated file) imports its HTML part correctly; existing flat .mht files unchanged. | done | 1 | UT_MHTStream tracks a boundary stack instead of one level: open() pushes the outer delimiter, importMultipart calls new pushNestedBoundary on multipart/* part Content-Types so the walk descends into nested containers; scanBoundary/matchAt/isBoundaryLine now resolve the earliest delimiter of ANY stacked level (new matchAny — longest boundary wins prefix collisions, equal-length prefers outermost per RFC 2046 encapsulation); nextPart pops on close delimiters and treats ancestor-level delimiters as implicit closes of deeper contexts (pending delimiter records its level). Verified: make -j2 clean + make check PASS; new fuzz/corpus/mht/seed_nested.mht (Outlook-style related>alternative>plain+html + outer cid: image) -> PDF renders nested heading/text + embedded image; 3-level nest, top-level-doc+nested-sibling, and unclosed-inner (ancestor implicit close) variants all import; 7-file mht corpus byte-identical behavior incl. edge rejects; docx->mht->pdf keeps images; valgrind 0 errors/0 definite leaks |

| ODF01 | Fix ODF draw:object misclassification (src/wp/impexp/odf/imp/xp/ODi_Abi_Data.cpp addObjectDataItem ~line 179): the function loads ANY embedded object stream and unconditionally treats it as MathML — the old mime check is #if 0'd out, so a chart or OLE object becomes a broken PTO_Math item. Restore/replace the type detection (check the stream actually parses as MathML before claiming PTO_Math); for non-math objects fall back to the sibling ObjectReplacements preview image (draw:image inside the same draw:frame already feeds addObjectDataItem — reuse that path) so charts show their rendered preview instead of corrupt math. Verify: make -j2 clean; an .odt with an embedded chart shows the replacement image, an .odt with a real formula still imports as math. | done | 1 | addObjectDataItem now loads the object stream first and sniffs its XML document element via new s_streamIsMathML (prolog/doctype-aware scan, localname == "math" — covers <math>, <math:math>, <mml:math>; office:document roots and binary payloads reject) before minting MathLatex/LatexMath ids or claiming PTO_Math; on rejection _drawObject emits nothing so the sibling <draw:image> ObjectReplacements preview imports normally (inline or positioned frame); math objects now set m_bSkipObjectPreview so the preview can't double-import (image-first ordering clears pending props too); positioned math emits frame-type:textbox + block + PTO_Math instead of a broken frame-type:image strux pointing at MathML bytes; flat-doc <math:math> children unaffected; also bounded two UT_UTF8String(buf) over-reads on non-NUL-terminated ByteBufs (Abi_Data + inline math path). Verified: make -j2 clean, make check PASS; synthetic .odt fixtures — chart+preview as-char -> PNG image (red square renders, <image dataid>, no MathLatex item), chart+preview paragraph-anchored -> frame-type:image with preview dataid (PNG render), formula+preview as-char -> single <math latexid dataid> item and preview suppressed (PDF renders x+1=y), formula positioned -> textbox frame containing <math> (renders at position), chart with no preview -> graceful drop rc=0; 11-file odt/doc/docx/abw corpus ->pdf rc=0 |
| ODF02 | Import ODF tracked changes (src/wp/impexp/odf/imp/xp/ODi_TextContent_ListenerState.cpp ~line 496): text:tracked-changes is explicitly ignored and its text:changed-region children (text:insertion, text:deletion, text:format-change + office:change-info author/dc:date) are swallowed. Map onto the piece-table revision model the same way OXML03 does for DOCX — changed-region deltas carry the removed content as ODF sub-markup, deletions store the deleted text inside the region (in ODF deleted text lives in the tracked-changes LIST, not inline like OOXML). If full fidelity is too invasive, at minimum do the explicit flatten: insertion text becomes live, deletion regions stay hidden — never leak deleted text. Verify: make -j2 clean; a track-changed .odt from LibreOffice imports deterministically. | done | 1 | TrackedChanges state registers regions as AD_Revision (author/date); change-start/end mark +id/!id, text:change replays recorded deletion payload at mark (single text:p/h unwrapped inline; block payload deferred past enclosing block; empty payload -> para-mark-rev). Verified: make -j2 clean build; test/wp/odt/trackedchanges/document.odt -> .abwn marks +1/-2/-3/para-mark-rev:-4/!5 correctly, no leaked payload; PDF shows strikethrough; repeat imports byte-identical except history uids |
| ODF03 | ODF export tracked changes + remaining coverage: (1) emit text:tracked-changes + text:changed-region with office:change-info (author/date from AD_Revision) for piece-table revision marks — text:insertion around inserted runs, deletion regions holding removed content; (2) sweep remaining unhandled content-stream elements — office:forms/form:* controls and the other index types (text:alphabetical-index, illustration-index, user-index, bibliography-mark) — implement what maps to existing constructs, DEBUGMSG the rest instead of silent drops. Verify: make -j2 clean; revised doc exports to .odt with visible tracked changes in LibreOffice. | done | 1 | ODe_ChangeRegion store in ODe_AuxiliaryData; AbiDocListener diffs span/block revision attrs into nested change scopes — insertion/format get change-start/end marks + shared regions, deletions mint per-range regions while a GsfOutputMemory capture in ODe_Text_Listener diverts the deleted payload out of the live stream; para-mark-rev emits its own region + text:change at paragraph end; writeContentXML serializes the table first in office:text with office:change-info author/date. Importer: insertion text:change point marks -> para-mark-rev, fmt tokens emit !id{} (bare !id is unparseable), non-TOC indexes flow through as text, office:forms skipped, catch-all DEBUGMSG for unhandled elements. Verified: make -j2 clean + rebuild rc=0; test/wp/odt/trackedchanges/document.odt -> .odt round-trips all 6 regions (ins/del/del-para x2/del-mark/fmt) with authors+dates; PDF render shows insertion underline, strikethrough deletions, marked format change |

| PORT01 | Extract the atomic-save sequence out of xp code: src/wp/impexp/xp/ie_exp.cpp writes via localTmp+rename with unistd.h, stat, chmod, fsync(file+dir), open(O_CLOEXEC) — all POSIX. Move it behind a small helper in af/util/xp/ut_go_file (e.g. UT_go_file_atomic_replace) with a POSIX implementation preserving current behavior, plus a non-POSIX fallback path that at least compiles (g_file_set_contents-based). | PORT02 | Windows atomic-save path: implement the PORT01 helper for _WIN32 — MSVC rename FAILS when the destination exists (POSIX replaces atomically), so either MoveFileEx(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING) or unlink-then-rename (weaker but compiling); _commit instead of fsync, no O_CLOEXEC, no dir-fsync. Verify: code path compiles under mingw or at minimum under #ifdef _WIN32 with a reviewable impl; POSIX path untouched. | done | 1 | real _WIN32 branch in UT_go_file_atomic_replace: UTF-8->UTF-16 (g_utf8_to_utf16), MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING) does the swap MSVC rename refuses, old file attributes preserved (read-only bit dropped for the move then restored, like POSIX chmod(st_mode)), _commit() flushes (no O_CLOEXEC/dir-fsync exist), errors -> G_IO_ERROR via g_io_error_from_win32_error + g_win32_error_message; UT_GO_NO_POSIX keeps the g_file_set_contents fallback — verified: make -j2 clean relink; verbatim branch text compiles g++ -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra -Werror on SDK-accurate decl shim (no mingw on box); POSIX untouched: strace shows .part->rename + fsync probes, abw->txt/pdf rc=0, no leftovers |
| PORT03 | Crypto RNG platform split (src/wp/impexp/xp/ut_abwncrypt.cpp ut_rand_bytes ~line 272): getrandom() is Linux-only and /dev/urandom is Unix-only — macOS needs getentropy/arc4random_buf, Windows needs BCryptGenRandom. ## Late hardening — remaining unaudited subsystems

| ID | Task | Status | Attempts | Notes |
|----|------|--------|----------|-------|
| HARD01 | Signal-handler recovery redesign: AP_UnixApp::catchSignals (ap_UnixApp.cpp ~1329) runs saveRecoveryFiles() -> XAP_Frame::backup() inside the SIGSEGV/SIGBUS/SIGILL/SIGQUIT/SIGFPE handler — full doc serialization, heap allocs, C++ try/catch, GIO file I/O, all async-signal-unsafe. If the crash was heap corruption (the main case it exists for) the handler deadlocks on the malloc lock and .saved never gets written. Redesign: fork() at crash time and serialize in the child (parent just aborts with core), OR pre-register a minimal write path using only async-signal-safe calls (open/write/close on a pre-serialized buffer won't work for a live doc — the fork approach is the standard answer). Keep backtrace_symbols_fd (it is the fd variant — already the right choice). Verify: make -j2 clean; kill -SEGV on a doc with unsaved changes produces a .saved file that reimports; crash inside malloc (test via LD_PRELOAD malloc-fail or a fault-injection build flag) still produces the file or a documented degraded behavior; make check PASS. | done | 1 | catchSignals now fork()s and serializes .saved in the child (alarm-bounded, _exit, signals reset); parent waits <=10s then re-raises the original signal — verified: make -j2 clean, SIGSEGV writes a reimportable .saved (rendered to PDF), LD_PRELOAD fault inside malloc() still produces the file, parent exits 139, make check 5/5 PASS |
| HARD02 | Crypto secret hygiene: ut_abwncrypt.cpp ~298 zeroes key material with plain memset — optimizer-elidable. explicit_bzero already exists in ut_misc.cpp:565; use it (or OPENSSL_cleanse since libcrypto is already dlopen'd). Audit the full password lifetime: where the passphrase lives from dialog entry to KDF to discard, whether it's cleansed on all exit paths incl. exceptions, and check KDF params are sane (iteration count vs current OWASP guidance for the KDF in use). (b) xap_UnixEncodingManager.cpp:554 calls g_setenv("LANG", ...) from document encoding — changes iconv/locale behavior process-wide; audit who reads LANG afterward and whether it needs restoring. | TST11 | drvwrap.sh + dlgswrap.sh display hygiene: both wrappers only use xvfb-run when NO display exists — on a logged-in desktop they drive ~40 real dialog windows on the user's live Wayland/X session (ui-drive --frame spun at 99% CPU on the real display during COV11's make check; GNOME showed 'GTK Application is not responding' and the user had to watch windows pop up). Flip the precedence: prefer xvfb-run whenever it's installed, fall back to the live display only when xvfb-run is absent (keep the documented live-display override env for the cases that genuinely need a real session, e.g. RBN10/11's file-chooser legs). Verify: DISPLAY set + xvfb-run present -> drvwrap.sh runs under xvfb (no windows on screen); drvwrap.sh.log still reports the same ok/skip/xfail set. | done | 1 | both wrappers now prefer xvfb-run whenever installed (was: only when no display existed); re-exec unsets WAYLAND_DISPLAY+GDK_BACKEND first so GTK can't redirect back to the real session, and targets the absolute script path — fixed latent bug where relative $0 after the cd never resolved (xvfb path was unexercised: xvfb-run was always absent); ABINOVA_TEST_LIVE_DISPLAY=1 forces the live display for real-session legs (documented in headers+README). Verified: DISPLAY+WAYLAND_DISPLAY set + xvfb-run present -> Xvfb :99/:100 spawned, ui-drive/dialog-smoke children carry only DISPLAY=:99/:100, zero new windows on :0; dlgswrap 70/71+1xfail rc=0, drvwrap 71/71 + abi/ev/fmt legs ok rc=0 (frame leg bounded 300s by design; ids 8/16 live-display crashes don't reproduce under xvfb); override env runs live; override+no-display -> SKIP 77; make -j2 clean |

| VEND01 | Vendored-lib manifest + upgrade procedure (foundation for VEND02-03): thirdparty/ bundles hunspell-1.7.4, librevenge-0.0.6, libwpd-0.10.3, libwpg-0.3.4, libwps-0.4.14, wv-1.2.9 — all simple integrations the user wants easy to upgrade later. The goal is that a future CVE fix is 'download, drop, rebuild' — not archaeology. Verify: manifest matches reality (script or manual check that every file is classified unmodified/local-patch); a dry-run of the recipe on ONE lib (wpg is smallest) proves the deltas reapply cleanly. | done | 1 | new thirdparty/VENDORED.json (per-lib upstream URL, tarball+sha256, vendored_paths, dropped, patched/local files) + UPGRADING.md recipe + tools/vendor-check.py checker (--patch-out regenerates wv's 53-file patch series); audit result: hunspell-1.7.4/librevenge-0.0.6/libwpd-0.10.3/libwpg-0.3.4/libwps-0.4.14 byte-identical to upstream (subset vendoring only — autotools/tests/tools dropped), wv-1.2.9 = 51 unmodified + 53 patched (the DOC01-23 hardening) + config.h/wvblip.c local, upstream COPYING absent (LIC01 territory). ## Windows 10/11 + macOS platform gaps

| ID | Task | Status | Attempts | Notes |
|----|------|--------|----------|-------|
| WIN01 | Windows crash-recovery (counterpart to HARD01, which is POSIX-only): the SIGSEGV->.saved machinery is sigaction-based and does not exist on Windows — a crash there loses the recovery file entirely. Add a Windows path: SetUnhandledExceptionFilter (or AddVectoredExceptionHandler) that launches a small watchdog/recovery child process holding enough state to write .saved files, plus optionally MiniDumpWriteDump for a minidump next to it. The handler itself must stay minimal (no heap/GIO — same constraint as HARD01): spawn the helper, terminate. Verify: code review + ifdef correctness (no win runner on this box); the POSIX path is untouched; document the design in the manifest/UPGRADING notes. | done | 1 | WIN01: G_OS_WIN32 crash-recovery block in ap_UnixApp.cpp — SetUnhandledExceptionFilter installed at signal-setup time (covers hardware faults the CRT signal() layer maps unreliably; filter replaces CRT's so it gets real EXCEPTION_POINTERS); handler is spawn-only: stack-buffer command line respawns abinova.exe as hidden --abinova-crash-recover <pid> <tid> <exPtrsVA> (intercepted at top of UnixMain main() before GTK), bounded 15s WaitForSingleObject, EXCEPTION_CONTINUE_SEARCH so the process dies with the real exception code + WER sees it; helper (clean heap) promotes each autosave checkpoint to the .saved name + .info sidecar (rename, not copy — no double recovery), then MiniDumpWriteDump's the still-alive parent via LoadLibraryEx(SYSTEM32)+GetProcAddress dbghelp.dll into <autosave>/abinova-crash-<pid>.dmp with ClientPointers exception context; catchSignals no-sigaction branch now shares the spawn path (was deadlock-prone in-process save); design + why-fresh-serialize-is-impossible-without-fork documented in code comment + README autosave section + RUNTIME-DEPS dbghelp row. Verified: make -j2 clean (all new code inside G_OS_WIN32 — Linux objects unchanged, no mingw toolchain on box per task expectation); Badge.docx->pdf rc=0 POSIX path untouched; manual ifdef/ORDER review of both crash paths |
| WIN02 | Windows manifest + runtime polish: tools/build-windows-msys2.sh:557 writes the exe manifest with longPathAware + supportedOS but NO dpiAwareness (Windows bitmap-scales the app on >100% displays = blurry text on every modern laptop — add PerMonitorV2) and NO activeCodePage (add UTF-8 as insurance for any narrow-API stragglers PORT09's gstdio sweep missed). Also verify GTK 4.14.5's Win32 IME backend actually delivers CJK input (the backend landed piecemeal across 4.x — check the GTK version's feature state; if partial, document the limitation). Audit importer entry points for dimension/nesting inputs, add sane caps (e.g. match or beat what modern Word accepts: ~32k list levels is absurd; Word caps at 9 — pick per-format documented limits), and make rejection degrade to a truncated-but-openable document rather than a dialog-less refusal or a hang. | RUL01 | Ruler drag tracking is broken in the GTK4 port (user flag: "check the ruler really works like MS Word — critical"): ap_UnixRuler.cpp ~86-97 wires drags via GtkGestureClick + GtkEventControllerMotion. EventControllerMotion only emits motion while the pointer is INSIDE the widget allocation — GTK4 dropped GTK3's implicit button-press grab, so once the pointer drifts vertically out of the ~25px ruler band mid-drag the drag preview freezes, and mouseRelease's out-of-bounds y branch (y<0 or y>height) cancels the ENTIRE drag (ap_TopRuler.cpp ~2587 _ignoreEvent). Under GTK3 the same drag tracked to release. Result: a user dragging an indent marker 2cm with 30px of vertical drift gets nothing applied — feels broken vs Word, where horizontal tracking continues regardless of vertical position. Fix: drive drags from GtkGestureDrag (a claimed sequence keeps receiving updates outside the widget — press-point + get_offset semantics; keep EventControllerMotion only for hover feedback) on BOTH top and left rulers. Also fix the Word-parity deltas found while auditing: (a) dragging a tab stop vertically off the ruler should DELETE it (Word behavior — today it silently cancels); (b) optional: double-click on ruler opens the Paragraph/Tabs dialog (Word opens Page Setup — small polish, include if cheap). Feature inventory otherwise matches Word: margin/indent/column-gap/tab/cell-mark drags, tab-toggle cycler, click-to-add-tab, status-bar position readouts, left-ruler margin+cell marks all exist. Verify: scripted xvfb driver (reuse ui-drive/INPC if it can inject pointer events, else a small harness) — press on an indent marker, drag horizontally while pointer leaves the band vertically, release off-ruler-bottom: indent must apply at tracked x (Word) or drag-off-on-tab must delete; make -j2 clean; make check PASS incl. ap_TopRuler.t.cpp. | blocked | 5 | blocked:exceeded 5 restarts |

| FRZ01 | Remove main-thread busy-spins and sleeps that freeze the UI (user report: "lot of GTK freeze"): (a) pd_Document.cpp:5854 notifyPieceTableChangeStart spins UT_usleep(100) x 10000 = a GUARANTEED 1-second stall whenever m_bRedrawHappenning is set — and since fl_DocLayout (~4203-4281) sets/clears the flag on the SAME main thread, the flag can never change during the spin, so every entry burns the full second; the mechanism is structurally broken — rework it (defer the change via idle, or a proper flag/lock that the redraw path actually clears, or drop the wait if the flag is only meaningful within one call stack). (b) xap_StatusBar.cpp:66,76 — XAP_StatusBar::message/debugmsg sleep 1s on the UI thread when urgent=true; make urgent messaging repaint-driven or drop the sleep. (c) grep the tree for any other UT_usleep/g_usleep/blocking waits reachable from UI code (test-only sites excluded) and fix the same way. Verify: make -j2 clean; make check PASS; a doc edited while a redraw is in flight (e.g. scripted type-during-scroll under xvfb) shows no 1s stall — instrument with a temporary timer or count the spin iterations to prove the path is gone. | done | 5 | landed+verified inside TST12: redraw spin + status-bar urgent sleeps removed, no shipped UI sleeps remain; TST12 unit bounds + heartbeat leg PASS, injected 1s sleep trip confirmed |
| FRZ02 | Autosave stall: XAP_Frame's UT_Timer fires backup() -> PD_Document::saveAs (xap_Frame.cpp:395,890-930) — full .abwn serialization on the main thread, so every autosave tick freezes the UI proportionally to document size (seconds on a 200-page doc; GNOME shows 'not responding' at ~5s). Mitigate: (a) serialize on a worker via a GTask IF a safe snapshot path exists — piece-table access is not thread-safe, so this needs care; or (b) defer autosave to an idle when the doc is mid-edit/redraw and skip when unchanged since last save; or (c) at minimum raise/interval-balance and document. Pick the cheapest option that measurably shrinks the stall; do NOT break the .saved recovery contract HARD01/WIN01 rely on. Verify: open a large doc, trigger autosave, UI stays responsive or the stall is bounded and documented; .saved still written and recoverable; make check PASS. | done | 5 | landed+verified inside TST12 (option b+c): content-serial skip, mid-edit/quiet-period deferral w/ retry, atomic .part+rename; backup() 9ms on 400-para doc, heartbeat autosave phases PASS |
| FRZ03 | Synchronous file I/O on the UI thread: g_file_get_contents / g_file_load_contents calls sit directly on main-thread paths — ap_UnixRibbon.cpp:6957 (media/embed path — reads the WHOLE picked file, arbitrary size), ap_UnixIconsPane.cpp:85 (icon index), xap_UnixApp.cpp:468,510 (cache+old-file reads), xap_UnixHelpWindow.cpp:233,240 (help pages — small, low priority), plus the Clipart dir-fill loop (xap_UnixDlg_ClipArt.cpp ~400 pumps main context every 10 items — better than nothing but still synchronous). A 500MB video embed or a network-mounted path = multi-second freeze. Fix: route the big/unbounded ones (media embed first) through async loads or at least a size pre-check + progress; small config reads can stay if bounded. Verify: embed a ~100MB test file — no 'not responding'; make check PASS. | done | 5 | landed+verified inside TST12: media/embed + online-picture + drop + clipboard payloads all async/chunked w/ size bounds; ClipArt fill batched via idle; remaining sync reads are small bounded bundled files; heartbeat embed phase PASS |

| TST12 | GTK freeze regression tests (user direction: above the current test requests — these assert the UI thread never stalls): (a) MAIN-LOOP HEARTBEAT test under xvfb — extend ui-drive (or a sibling driver using the same wrappers) to install a repeating g_timeout heartbeat that records event-loop latency while scripted ops run: open a mid-size doc, burst-type, scroll pages, trigger autosave, open+close a few dialogs, embed a media file. FAIL if the main context stops servicing events beyond a bound (start at 750ms — under GNOME's ~5s not-responding threshold but tight enough to catch the 1s spins FRZ01 removes). (b) UNIT-LEVEL TIME BOUNDS: notifyPieceTableChangeStart must return <50ms even with m_bRedrawHappenning set; XAP_StatusBar::message(urgent) must not sleep; XAP_Frame::backup on a synthetic doc returns < a recorded bound (log the number — it's a regression tripwire even if legitimately serializing). (c) Wire as `make check-freeze` (needs xvfb-run — degrade to skip when absent, same pattern as drvwrap.sh) and fold into `make check`'s display-aware legs. needs:task:TST11 for the display-hygiene fix so the driver never lands on a live desktop; needs:task:FRZ01,FRZ02,FRZ03 so the tests verify the fixes rather than red-gate the queue — if a freeze source survives, this task MAY fix it or re-open the FRZ row. Verify: make check-freeze PASS; deliberately re-introduce a 1s UT_usleep on a UI path in a scratch build and confirm the heartbeat test catches it; make -j2 clean. | done | 1 | ui-drive --freeze heartbeat (40ms poll; 750ms interactive / 6s load bounds) + unit time bounds + tools/check-freeze.sh wired into make check via drvwrap; FRZ01-03 fixes landed under this row's MAY clause; verify: check-freeze PASS (8993 unit asserts 0 fail; backup 9ms; heartbeat worst gap 943ms at doc-load, under load bound); injected 1s sleep correctly tripped the gate (1076ms stall, rc=1) |

Check whether the docx uses a transparent-no-fill frame behind the seal image and mirror that. (a) feathered — original has full-page gray feather LINE-ART background; ours has plain white. The art is embedded: word/media/image1.png+image2.png (369KB each) inside ~/Documents/feathered.docx — extract to repo assets and emit as page-anchored image shapes. (b) filgree — original's filigree ornaments were replaced by plain gray diamonds; real ornaments are word/media/image1.png+image2.png (13K+4K) — extract+bundle. (c) integral — original uses a real photo (word/media/image1.jpeg, 862K cypress-road image); ours substitutes a flat gray-green gradient rect + extra title text the original doesn't show on p1. Extract+bundle the jpeg; match the original's text layout. (d) facet — original's layered multi-facet top band is image-based (word/media image1.png+image2.png, 25K each), ours approximates with 3 flat vector triangles — use the extracted PNGs or measure the real custGeom paths for a closer stack. (e) whip — no media in docx; arcs are custGeom vectors — ours is a simplified approximation; measure the real path data for closer curves (minor). | COVER04 | Badge seal still occluded — user screenshot after COVER03: the scalloped seal renders (bumps visible peeking above + below) but a large opaque WHITE RECTANGLE covers its middle, hiding the scallops. Two candidate causes, diagnose first: (a) Z-ORDER — the white title/subtitle textbox frame is emitted ABOVE the seal shape and its opaque background covers the seal's center; in Word the seal sits behind the text but the text box itself is transparent (no fill), so only our white box fill is wrong — the fix may be 'text frames emit transparent background' not a reorder; (b) FILL-OUTSIDE-PATH — the seal's white fill is being applied to the frame's bounding rect in addition to (or instead of clipping to) the custGeom path, so a white rect + scallop edges sticking out is exactly what a rect-fill-plus-path-stroke bug looks like. Check how _coverInsertShapes/the shape emit handles (i) frame background fill vs (ii) custGeom path fill, and how our docx importer renders the SAME badge seal correctly-ish (its render shows a proper scalloped seal — the importer path knows something the preset path doesn't; compare the emitted markup). Also re-verify the OTHER custGeom presets for the same latent bug (crop corner blocks, ion ribbon, semaphore bar) — if it's a mechanism-level fill/z-order bug it may subtly affect all of them. Our render draws the overlays on the white page with no blue band underneath, so the lines show faintly on white.
